--- Commit Message ---
LU-18456 tcu: move regular files into trash upon last unlink

"last unlink" moving into trash can will create a directory named
after the parent FID in the trash dir for the corresponding MDT.
Then, if/when the parent directory is also removed, the FID-named
directory in trash will rename to use its original file name (and
also inherit the actual FID or other xattrs like crypt, selinux,
etc) from the now-deleted parent.

This can avoid having to move all of the deleted files over to the
"real" deleted parent.

This patch implements the part of moving regular files into trash
upon last unlink.

Add sanityn/test_117b to verfiy it works as expected.

Change-Id: Iec59fbb3719c52c4f84c97c74e40ee0cfadad75d
Signed-off-by: Qian Yingjin <qian@ddn.com>

--- Message (Andreas Dilger, 2025-03-29 19:26:51.000000000) ---
Patch Set 4:

This should create and use a per-UID directory in the Trash directory to isolate user files.  The UID should be the UID of the file/directory being deleted, not the euid of the process deleting the file. This functionality should always be enabled, since it is a security feature, since some files/dirs in a user's subdirectories may not have proper permissions to restrict access. 

For clients in a nodemap, there should be a top-level directory with the nodemap name above the UID directory, with UID=mapped root UID, but 1777 permission (sticky directory, like /tmp, so that only users can delete their own files). The UID directory *name* should be the unmapped tenant UID (since this is what clients will see), and the *owner* should be the mapped UID from the nodemap (which is what will control file access). 

The nodemap handling could be in a separate patch, but I think the UID handling needs to be in this patch, since it will affect the on-disk location of all deleted files.

--- Message (Qian Yingjin, 2025-03-30 03:25:59.000000000) ---
Patch Set 4:

I am afraid this may downgrade the performance of unlink operations.
It needs two extra lookup or creation in the unlink transactions. (one for "UID", another for "pFID", or even the already existed "pFID" for the deleting directory).

We would better to have a better mechanism to accelerate these extra lookup, I think.

--- Message (Andreas Dilger, 2025-03-31 09:10:34.000000000) ---
Patch Set 4:

> I am afraid this may downgrade the performance of unlink
 > operations.
 > It needs two extra lookup or creation in the unlink transactions.
 > (one for "UID", another for "pFID", or even the already existed
 > "pFID" for the deleting directory).
 > 
 > We would better to have a better mechanism to accelerate these
 > extra lookup, I think.

The nodemap itself could cache the top of the trash directory so that it doesn't need to be looked up each time?  Possibly the directory object itself could cache a pointer to the pFID stub directory so that it only needs to do a single lookup when the first file in a directory is unlinked, and then the remaining unlinks would find this for free.

--- Message (Qian Yingjin, 2025-04-01 02:50:22.000000000) ---
Patch Set 4:

Should we keep two version w/ or w/o UID under trash to see the impact on the performance?

--- Message (Andreas Dilger, 2025-04-02 18:32:32.000000000) ---
Patch Set 4:

> Should we keep two version w/ or w/o UID under trash to see the
 > impact on the performance?

Sure, measuring the difference in performance is useful.  I think if each MDT directory object caches a pointer to the stub directory object (or pFID) then the overhead of creating/looking up the per-UID directory should be relatively small, depending on how many files are deleted in each directory.

It would be useful to benchmark some different combinations:
- trash can disabled, single trash dir, per UID trash dir
- 1, 10, 100 files per directory

--- Message (Qian Yingjin, 2025-04-09 04:00:01.000000000) ---
Patch Set 4:

Can we implement per-UID trash directory in a separate patch?
As we do not enabled Trash Can feature yet.

--- Message (Andreas Dilger, 2025-04-09 05:07:08.000000000) ---
Patch Set 4:

> Can we implement per-UID trash directory in a separate patch?
 > As we do not enabled Trash Can feature yet.

Do you think this is a significant amount of work to implement?  I'm OK to implementing this in a later patch (e.g. on top of this one), but it must be part of the initial feature release.

I think this is a relatively small amount of work:
- in the .lustre/Trash/MDTxxxx/ directory do a lookup of inode->i_uid of the file being deleted before doing the pFID lookup
- create that directory (once per user per filesystem) if it doesn't exist, with 0700 permissions
- use the UID/ directory for pFID lookup
